─ Worked for 2m 38s ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

• Findings

  - --workflow-id as UUID targets artifacts.workflow_id (internal PK) but API/
    public ID is workflows.workflow_id (string); this will be confusing and
    likely wrong in real use. See apps/api/infrastructure/db/models/workflow.py.
  - Defaulting to “All workflows” can overcount and misreport Gate A; the gate
    is per‑workflow and expects exactly 36 docs for a single workflow. See Gate
    A in docs/spec/3_solver-technical-spec.md.
  - Adding count_methodology_docs_grouped in apps/api/infrastructure/db/
    repositories/artifact.py expands core code during verification; not required
    for P6.1 and adds surface area late in the cycle.
  - Counting alone is insufficient for “valid content” in Appendix A; you should
    also ensure content_markdown is present/non‑empty and filter
    artifact_type='methodology_doc' and pass_type='definition'. See apps/api/
    infrastructure/db/models/artifact.py.

  What’s good

  - Scope stays within P6.1 and aligns with the 3×4×3 methodology structure; doc
    types/versions are correct.
  - Output/reporting and exit codes are sensible for a CLI gate check.

  Best path to meet the objective

  - Keep P6.1 self‑contained: implement tools/verify_methodology.py without
    modifying apps/api/infrastructure/db/repositories/artifact.py by using
    ArtifactRepository.list_for_workflow plus in‑script grouping.
  - Accept external workflow_id (string), resolve it to internal UUID via
    WorkflowRepository.get_by_workflow_id; if not provided, select the most
    recent workflow or fail when ambiguous.
  - Enforce filters and uniqueness per step/type/version and validate non‑empty
    content_markdown to satisfy Appendix A.

  correct |

    The public API uses string workflow IDs like "wf-abc123". Users will copy
  these from API responses. My plan asks for the internal UUID which users
  won't have. Fix: accept string, resolve via WorkflowRepository.

    2. "All Workflows" Default — Agree, must fix

    | My Plan                           | Co-Developer-1
  | Verdict                   |

  |-----------------------------------|----------------------------------------
  -----------|---------------------------|
    | Default to scanning all workflows | Gate A is per-workflow (36 docs for
  ONE workflow) | Co-Developer-1 is correct |

    Aggregating across workflows is misleading. A workflow with 18 docs +
  another with 18 docs = 36 total but neither passes Gate A. Fix: require
  --workflow-id OR auto-select most recent workflow (with warning).

    3. Adding Method to ArtifactRepository — Agree, prefer self-contained

    | My Plan                                                 | Co-Developer-1
  | Verdict                                |

  |---------------------------------------------------------|------------------
  -------------------------------------|---------------------------------------
  -|

  list_for_workflow() + in-script grouping | Co-Developer-1 has the better
  approach |

    Rationale:
    - P6 is verification phase, not feature development
    - Adding methods to production code increases surface area and test burden
    - A verification tool should be self-contained and use existing APIs
    - Python grouping of ~36 artifacts is trivial performance-wise

    4. Content Validation Beyond Counting — Agree, must add

    | My Plan         | Co-Developer-1                         | Verdict
  |

  |-----------------|----------------------------------------|-----------------
  ----------|
    | Count rows only | Validate content_markdown is non-empty | Co-Developer-1

  also enforce:
    - artifact_type = 'methodology_doc'
    - pass_type = 'definition'
    - content_markdown IS NOT NULL AND content_markdown != ''

    ---
    Revised Approach

    CHANGES FROM ORIGINAL PLAN:

    1. NO modifications to apps/api/infrastructure/db/repositories/artifact.py
       - Use ArtifactRepository.list_for_workflow() and filter/group in Python

    2. Accept --workflow-id as STRING (external ID)
       - Resolve to internal UUID via WorkflowRepository.get_by_workflow_id()
       - If not provided: auto-select most recent workflow with warning

    3. Validate content, not just existence
       - Filter: artifact_type='methodology_doc', pass_type='definition'
       - Check: content_markdown is non-empty
       - Report: "Step 1 / data_sheet / v2: MISSING" or "EMPTY"

• I agree with the Senior_Developer’s revised approach. It aligns with the
  directives and fixes the spec‑critical issues.

  Why I agree

  - Workflow ID handling: Using external workflow_id (string) matches API usage
    and avoids hidden internal UUID assumptions; this is consistent with
    WorkflowRepository.get_by_workflow_id which already exists.
  - Single‑workflow scope: Gate A is explicitly per workflow; defaulting to “all
    workflows” can yield false positives.
  - Self‑contained tooling: P6 is verification; avoid expanding production
    repository APIs late in the cycle.
  - Content validation: Appendix A expects “valid content,” so empty
    content_markdown should fail.

  Clarifications / tweaks

  - The “auto‑select most recent workflow” default should warn loudly or require
    --workflow-id to avoid accidental verification of the wrong workflow. A
    strict requirement is safer for Gate A compliance.
  - Ensure the tool filters: artifact_type='methodology_doc',
    pass_type='definition', step_number in {1,2,3}, and document_type/
    document_version present.

  I recommend updating the plan to require --workflow-id or a --latest flag,
  then proceed with the revised one‑file tool.

─ Worked for 47s ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

• Findings

  - High: Missing workflow scoping and external ID handling; Gate A is per
    workflow and the tool must accept the public workflow_id string and resolve
    to the internal UUID (Appendix A + API usage). Aggregating or skipping the
    filter can falsely pass.
  - High: No content validation; counting rows alone doesn’t satisfy “valid
    content” in Appendix A. Must check content_markdown is non‑empty.
  - Medium: Import/path guidance is brittle; “add ../ to sys.path” plus
    apps.api.* imports diverge from the repo’s established pattern (sys.path →
    apps/api then config, infrastructure...), which risks import failures.
  - Medium: “Verify by --help” isn’t a gate check; must actually query and
    evaluate 36 combos for the workflow.
  - Low: Direct SQLAlchemy in the tool is acceptable, but avoid duplicating
    logic if existing repository methods already cover the need.

  Negative Patterns to Avoid

  - Verifying across all workflows or requiring internal UUIDs from users.
  - Treating existence of rows as proof of validity; empty docs must fail.
  - Import hacks that change module naming conventions and make tools fragile.


› So generate a comprehensive consolidated feedback for the Senior Developer.

    Assessment       |

    |----------------------------------|--------------------------|------------
  ---
    ------|
      | Optional, with --latest fallback | Required (string)        | Agree
  with
    required |

      Rationale for requiring --workflow-id:
      - Explicit > implicit — user knows exactly what they're verifying
      - Avoids ambiguity about "latest" semantics (by created_at? updated_at?)
      - Simpler implementation, fewer edge cases
      - If --latest is needed, it can be a future enhancement

      Content Validation Emphasis

      Co-Developer-1 is explicit: "Empty docs must fail."

      This means the tool must check:
      # Not just existence, but valid content
      if artifact.content_markdown is None or artifact.content_markdown.strip()
  ==
    "":
          status = "EMPTY"  # Fails Gate A

      Uniqueness Constraint

      New detail: "enforce exactly one per combination"

      This catches a subtle bug — if somehow two v1 data_sheets exist for Step
  1,
    that's anomalous. The tool should:
      - Expect exactly 1 artifact per (step, doc_type, version) tuple
      - Flag duplicates as warnings or errors

      ---
      Final Perspective

      I fully agree with Co-Developer-1's recommended shape. It is:

      1. Minimal — single file, no production code changes
      2. Explicit — requires workflow-id, no magic defaults
      3. Thorough — validates content, not just counts
      4. Clear — defined exit codes and output format

      Implementation Summary

      tools/verify_methodology.py

      INPUTS:
        --workflow-id   STRING (required) - public workflow ID
        --steps         INT... (optional) - default: 1 2 3
        --database-url  STRING (optional) - override from settings

      BEHAVIOR:
        1. Resolve workflow_id string → internal UUID
        2. Query artifacts WHERE:
           - workflow_id = <resolved UUID>
           - artifact_type = 'methodology_doc'
           - pass_type = 'definition'
           - step_number IN (1, 2, 3)
        3. Group by (step_number, document_type, document_version)
        4. For each of 36 expected combinations:
           - MISSING: no artifact found
           - EMPTY: artifact exists but content_markdown is null/blank
           - OK: artifact exists with content
           - DUPLICATE: more than one artifact (warning)
        5. Exit 0 if all 36 are OK, else exit 1

      EXIT CODES:
        0 - PASS (36 valid docs)
        1 - FAIL (missing/empty docs)
        2 - ERROR (database connection)
        3 - ERROR (workflow not found)

      OUTPUT:
        Grid showing status per step/type/version + summary

     P6.1 Plan: Gate A Verification Tool (Final)

     Objective

     Create tools/verify_methodology.py - CLI tool to verify 36 methodology
  docs
    exist with valid content (Gate
      A criterion).

     Gate A Criterion

     - 3 steps × 4 document types × 3 versions = 36 methodology documents
     - Document types: data_sheet, todo_list, guidance, detailed_procedure
     - Document versions: v1, v2, v3
     - Steps (MVP): 1, 2, 3
     - Content must be non-empty (not just existence)

     Files to Create

     | File                        | Action | Purpose                 |
     |-----------------------------|--------|-------------------------|
     | tools/verify_methodology.py | Create | Self-contained CLI tool |

     No modifications to production code in apps/api/.

     CLI Specification

     Arguments

     | Argument       | Type   | Required | Default       | Description
    |

    |----------------|--------|----------|---------------|---------------------
  ---
    --------------|
     | --workflow-id  | STRING | Yes      | —             | Public workflow ID
    (e.g., wf-abc123) |
     | --steps        | INT... | No       | 1 2 3         | Steps to verify
    |
     | --database-url | STRING | No       | From settings | Override DB
  connection
    |
     | --verbose / -v | Flag   | No       | False         | Show detailed
  output
    |

     Exit Codes

     | Code | Meaning                                     |
     |------|---------------------------------------------|
     | 0    | PASS - All 36 docs found with valid content |
     | 1    | FAIL - Missing or empty docs                |
     | 2    | ERROR - Database connection failed          |
     | 3    | ERROR - Workflow not found                  |

     Behavior

     1. Resolve workflow ID: Accept public workflow_id string → resolve to
    internal UUID via WorkflowRepository
     2. Query artifacts with filters:
       - workflow_id = <resolved UUID>
       - artifact_type = 'methodology_doc'
       - pass_type = 'definition'
       - step_number IN (1, 2, 3)
     3. Group by (step_number, document_type, document_version)
     4. Validate each of 36 combinations:
       - MISSING - No artifact found
       - EMPTY - Artifact exists but content_markdown is null/blank
       - OK - Artifact exists with valid content
       - DUPLICATE - More than one artifact (warning)
     5. Exit 0 only if all 36 are OK

     Output Format

     Gate A Verification Report
     ==========================
     Workflow: wf-abc123
     Steps: 1, 2, 3

     Step 1: problem_definition
     --------------------------
     Document Type        v1       v2       v3
     data_sheet           OK       OK       OK
     todo_list            OK       OK       OK
     guidance             OK       MISSING  OK
     detailed_procedure   OK       OK       EMPTY

     Step 2: requirements
     --------------------------
     ...

     Summary
     =======
     Expected: 36
     OK:       34
     Missing:  1
     Empty:    1
     Status:   FAIL

     Implementation Approach

     Self-Contained Design

     - Use existing ArtifactRepository.list_for_workflow()
     - Filter and group in Python (no new repository methods)
     - Import via sys.path pointing to apps/api

     Key Query Logic

     # Fetch all artifacts for workflow
     artifacts = await artifact_repo.list_for_workflow(workflow_uuid)

     # Filter to methodology docs
     methodology_docs = [
         a for a in artifacts
         if a.artifact_type == "methodology_doc"
         and a.pass_type == PassType.DEFINITION
         and a.step_number in steps_to_check
     ]

     # Group by (step, type, version) and validate content
     for step in steps:
         for doc_type in DocumentType:
             for version in DocumentVersion:
                 matches = [a for a in methodology_docs if ...]
                 if len(matches) == 0:
                     status = "MISSING"
                 elif not matches[0].content_markdown or not
    matches[0].content_markdown.strip():
                     status = "EMPTY"
                 else:
                     status = "OK"

     Verification Command

     python tools/verify_methodology.py --workflow-id wf-abc123
     # Exit 0 if all 36 docs exist with content

     Dependencies

     - apps/api/infrastructure/db/repositories/artifact.py (existing, read-
  only)
     - apps/api/infrastructure/db/repositories/workflow.py (existing, read-
  only)
     - apps/api/infrastructure/db/models/enums.py (DocumentType,
  DocumentVersion,
    PassType)
     - apps/api/config.py (settings.postgres_url)

    </information only>
    No action required.

     P6.2 Plan: Gate B Schema Validation Tool

     Objective

     Create tools/validate_schemas.py - CLI tool to validate Pass 2 step
  packages
    against JSON schemas (Gate B
     criterion: schema validation).

     Note: Per docs/DECISIONS.md, trace validation (tools/verify_traces.py and
    /workflows/{id}/traceability
     endpoint) is deferred. This slice focuses ONLY on schema validation.

     Gate B Criterion (Schema Portion)

     - Step 1-3 Pass 2 packages exist with valid schemas
     - Each package (ProblemDefinitionPackage, RequirementsPackage,
    ObjectivesPackage) validates against its
     JSON schema
     - Packages stored in content_jsonb field with
  artifact_type="step_package",
    pass_type="execution"

     File to Create

     | File                      | Action | Purpose
    |

    |---------------------------|--------|-------------------------------------
  ---
    -------|
     | tools/validate_schemas.py | Create | Self-contained CLI tool for schema
    validation |

     No modifications to production code in apps/api/.

     CLI Specification

     Arguments

     | Argument       | Type   | Required | Default       | Description
    |

    |----------------|--------|----------|---------------|---------------------
  ---
    --------------|
     | --workflow-id  | STRING | Yes      | —             | Public workflow ID
    (e.g., wf-abc123) |
     | --steps        | INT... | No       | 1 2 3         | Steps to validate
    |
     | --database-url | STRING | No       | From settings | Override DB
  connection
    |
     | --verbose / -v | Flag   | No       | False         | Show detailed
    validation errors      |

     Exit Codes

     | Code | Meaning                                                  |
     |------|----------------------------------------------------------|
     | 0    | PASS - All expected step packages found and schema-valid |
     | 1    | FAIL - Missing packages or schema validation errors      |
     | 2    | ERROR - Database connection failed                       |
     | 3    | ERROR - Workflow not found                               |

     Behavior

     1. Resolve workflow ID: Accept public workflow_id string → resolve to
    internal UUID via WorkflowRepository
     2. Query step packages for each step:
       - Use ArtifactRepository.get_step_package(workflow_id, step_name)
       - Filter: artifact_type = "step_package", pass_type = "execution"
     3. Validate each package against JSON schema:
       - Load schema via application/schemas.get_schema_for_step(step_name)
       - Use jsonschema.Draft7Validator to validate content_jsonb
     4. Report status per package:
       - VALID - Package exists and passes schema validation
       - INVALID - Package exists but fails schema validation (list errors)
       - MISSING - No package found for step
     5. Exit 0 only if all requested steps have VALID packages

     Output Format

     Gate B Schema Validation Report
     ===============================
     Workflow: wf-abc123
     Steps: 1, 2, 3

     Step 1: problem_definition
     --------------------------
     Package: ProblemDefinitionPackage
     Revision: 1
     Status: VALID

     Step 2: requirements
     --------------------------
     Package: RequirementsPackage
     Revision: 2
     Status: INVALID
     Errors:
       - path: ['requirements', 0, 'id']
         message: 'INVALID-ID' does not match '^(FR|NFR|CR|IR)-\\d{3}$'

     Step 3: objectives
     --------------------------
     Status: MISSING

     Summary
     =======
     Expected: 3
     Valid:    1
     Invalid:  1
     Missing:  1
     Status:   FAIL

     Implementation Approach

     Self-Contained Design (Following verify_methodology.py Pattern)

     #!/usr/bin/env python3
     import sys, os
     sys.path.insert(0, os.path.join(os.path.dirname(__file__), "..", "apps",
    "api"))

     from infrastructure.db.models import StepName, PassType
     from infrastructure.db.repositories import ArtifactRepository,
    WorkflowRepository
     from application.schemas import get_schema_for_step
     import jsonschema

     Key Query Logic

     # Get step package (latest revision)
     artifact = await artifact_repo.get_step_package(
         workflow_id=workflow_uuid,
         step_name=step_name,  # StepName.PROBLEM_DEFINITION, etc.
         revision=None,  # Gets latest
     )

     if artifact is None:
         status = "MISSING"
     elif artifact.content_jsonb is None:
         status = "EMPTY"
     else:
         # Validate against schema
         schema = get_schema_for_step(step_name)
         validator = jsonschema.Draft7Validator(schema)
         errors = list(validator.iter_errors(artifact.content_jsonb))
         status = "VALID" if not errors else "INVALID"

     Step-to-Schema Mapping

     | Step | StepName Enum      | Package Type             | Schema File
    |

    |------|--------------------|--------------------------|-------------------
  ---
    ----------|
     | 1    | PROBLEM_DEFINITION | ProblemDefinitionPackage |
    problem_definition.schema.json |
     | 2    | REQUIREMENTS       | RequirementsPackage      |
    requirements.schema.json       |
     | 3    | OBJECTIVES         | ObjectivesPackage        |
    objectives.schema.json         |

     Verification Commands

     # Help
     python tools/validate_schemas.py --help

     # Validate all steps for workflow
     python tools/validate_schemas.py --workflow-id wf-abc123

     # Verbose output with errors
     python tools/validate_schemas.py --workflow-id wf-abc123 --verbose

     # Specific steps only
     python tools/validate_schemas.py --workflow-id wf-abc123 --steps 1 2

     Dependencies (Read-Only)

     - apps/api/infrastructure/db/repositories/artifact.py - get_step_package()
     - apps/api/infrastructure/db/repositories/workflow.py -
  get_by_workflow_id()
     - apps/api/infrastructure/db/models/ - StepName, PassType
     - apps/api/application/schemas.py - get_schema_for_step()
     - packages/contracts/*.schema.json - Step 1-3 JSON schemas
     - apps/api/config.py - settings.postgres_url

     Not In Scope (Deferred per docs/DECISIONS.md)

     - tools/verify_traces.py - Traceability link verification
     - /workflows/{id}/traceability endpoint
     - Trace link validation (coverage_map, trace_map checks)

     Test Scenarios

     1. --help shows usage
     2. Non-existent workflow returns exit code 3
     3. Workflow with valid packages returns exit code 0
     4. Workflow with missing packages returns exit code 1
     5. Workflow with invalid packages returns exit code 1 + errors in verbose
    mode

    </Senior Developer>

─ Worked for 1m 40s ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

• - Critical: The plan imports StepName from infrastructure.db.models, but
    application.schemas.get_schema_for_step keys are domain.state.StepName;
    passing the wrong enum will raise KeyError and mark schemas invalid. Fix by
    using domain.state.StepName for schema lookup or mapping step numbers to
    both enums explicitly. References: apps/api/application/schemas.py, apps/
    api/infrastructure/db/models/enums.py.
  - High: ArtifactRepository.get_step_package doesn’t filter pass_type, so you
    must enforce artifact.pass_type == PassType.EXECUTION in the tool to avoid
    validating the wrong row if data is polluted. Reference: apps/api/
    infrastructure/db/repositories/artifact.py.
  - Medium: The plan doesn’t validate package_type matches the step (e.g.,
    ProblemDefinitionPackage). It’s not strictly required by schema, but it’s a
    low‑cost integrity check that tightens Gate B. Reference: apps/api/
    application/artifact_service.py.
  - Medium: CLI requires --workflow-id, but docs/spec/0_SOLVER-Development-
    Directive.md shows python tools/validate_schemas.py --steps 1 2 3. Requiring
    the ID is safer and aligns with per‑workflow semantics, but it’s a doc
    mismatch that should be called out (or add an explicit --latest if
    approved).
  - Low: Consider deduping --steps to avoid inflated expected counts, mirroring
    the fix in tools/verify_methodology.py.

  Best path to meet the objective

  - Keep the tool self‑contained in tools/validate_schemas.py, no production
    code changes, and keep trace validation deferred per docs/DECISIONS.md.
  - Use WorkflowRepository.get_by_workflow_id() to resolve the public ID, then
    either get_step_package plus explicit pass_type/package_type checks, or
    list_for_workflow with Python filtering for clarity.
  - Validate content_jsonb with jsonschema.Draft7Validator and emit clear
    per‑step status (VALID/INVALID/MISSING/EMPTY) plus error details under
    --verbose.
  - In the slice report, state Gate B is schema‑verified but trace verification
    remains blocked until deferred tooling is approved.

  This plan is directionally correct and within scope; the enum mismatch is the
  primary blocker to correctness.

  avoiding production changes, deferring traceability, and explicit workflow ID
  use. However, I see issues with enum mismatches between domain.state.StepName
  and infrastructure.db.models.StepName that could cause runtime or query

  validation of incorrect data. Their use of infrastructure.db.models.Workflow

  omit checks for package_type, and they don't flag a conflict between requiring
  --workflow-id and directive docs that don’t expect it. Negative patterns

  filters like pass_type, and potentially querying raw models instead of
  repositories. Overall, their plan aligns on structure but needs more careful
  adherence to data types, filtering, and directive consistency.

› For information only. <information only> ⏺ Evaluation of Co-Developer-1
    Feedback

      Co-Developer-1 raises several excellent points. Let me analyze each:

      1. Query Strategy: get_step_package() vs list_for_workflow() + filtering

      Co-Developer-1's approach:
      artifacts = await artifact_repo.list_for_workflow(workflow_uuid)
      step_packages = [a for a in artifacts
          if a.artifact_type == "step_package"
          and a.pass_type == PassType.EXECUTION]

      My plan's approach:
      artifact = await artifact_repo.get_step_package(workflow_id, step_name)

      My perspective: For a verification tool, Co-Developer-1's explicit
  filtering
    approach is superior. Verification tools should be transparent about what
    they're checking - relying on repository method internals is less
  auditable.
    I'll adopt this approach.

      2. StepName Enum Confusion

      Co-Developer-1 warns: "Don't mix domain.state.StepName and
    infrastructure.db.models.StepName"

      My perspective: This is a critical catch! Looking at the code:
      - application/schemas.py imports from domain.state import StepName
      - infrastructure/db/models/ has its own StepName enum
      - These may be the same enum or may differ

      Resolution: Use explicit step_number → domain.state.StepName mapping for
    schema lookup:
      from domain.state import StepName as DomainStepName

      STEP_NUMBER_TO_DOMAIN_STEP = {
          1: DomainStepName.PROBLEM_DEFINITION,
          2: DomainStepName.REQUIREMENTS,
          3: DomainStepName.OBJECTIVES,
      }

      3. Status Values: EMPTY

      Co-Developer-1 adds: EMPTY status for content_jsonb = None

      My perspective: Agreed. A package could exist with null content - this is
    distinct from MISSING (no artifact) and INVALID (failed validation). Adding
    EMPTY maintains parity with verify_methodology.py.

      4. Package Type Mismatch

      Co-Developer-1 suggests: "optionally flag package_type mismatch"

      Question: Should mismatch be FAIL or warning?

      My perspective: Treat as FAIL (INVALID). If step 2 has package_type =
    "ProblemDefinitionPackage" instead of "RequirementsPackage", that's a
    structural error indicating data corruption or bug - not a valid state.

      5. Co-Developer-1's Questions

      | Question                                 | My Recommendation
    |

    |------------------------------------------|-------------------------------
  ---
    ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
  ---
    --------------------------|
      | --workflow-id required vs --latest flag? | Keep required. Verification
    tools should target explicit workflows. A --latest flag adds complexity
    without clear benefit for Gate B.    |
      | Package type mismatch: FAIL or warning?  | FAIL (INVALID). Structural
    integrity matters.
    |
      | Latest revision only or all revisions?   | Latest only (default). Gate
  B
    asks "does valid package exist?" not "are all revisions valid?" Could add
    --all-revisions later if needed. |

      ---
      Updated Plan Recommendations

      1. Use explicit filtering (list_for_workflow() + filter) instead of
    get_step_package()
      2. Use domain.state.StepName explicitly for schema lookup with
  step_number
    mapping
      3. Add EMPTY status for null content_jsonb
      4. Validate package_type matches expected value (mismatch = INVALID)
      5. Keep --workflow-id required
      6. Validate latest revision only (per step)

         P6.2 Plan: Gate B Schema Validation Tool (Revised)

         Objective

         Create tools/validate_schemas.py - CLI tool to validate Pass 2 step
    packages against JSON schemas
         (Gate B criterion: schema validation).

         Note: Per docs/DECISIONS.md, trace validation (tools/verify_traces.py
  and
         /workflows/{id}/traceability endpoint) is deferred. This slice focuses
    ONLY on schema validation.

         Gate B Criterion (Schema Portion)

         - Step 1-3 Pass 2 packages exist with valid schemas
         - Each package (ProblemDefinitionPackage, RequirementsPackage,
    ObjectivesPackage) validates against
          its JSON schema
         - Packages stored in content_jsonb field with
    artifact_type="step_package", pass_type="execution"

         File to Create

         | File                      | Action | Purpose
    |

    |---------------------------|--------|-------------------------------------
  ---
    -------|
         | tools/validate_schemas.py | Create | Self-contained CLI tool for
  schema
    validation |

         No modifications to production code in apps/api/.

         CLI Specification

         Arguments

         | Argument       | Type   | Required | Default       | Description
    |

    |----------------|--------|----------|---------------|---------------------
  ---
    --------------|
         | --workflow-id  | STRING | Yes      | —             | Public workflow
  ID
    (e.g., wf-abc123) |
         | --steps        | INT... | No       | 1 2 3         | Steps to
  validate
    |
         | --database-url | STRING | No       | From settings | Override DB
    connection               |
         | --verbose / -v | Flag   | No       | False         | Show detailed
    validation errors      |

         Exit Codes

         | Code | Meaning                                                  |
         |------|----------------------------------------------------------|
         | 0    | PASS - All expected step packages found and schema-valid |
         | 1    | FAIL - Missing, empty, or invalid packages               |
         | 2    | ERROR - Database connection failed                       |
         | 3    | ERROR - Workflow not found                               |

         Behavior

         1. Resolve workflow ID: Accept public workflow_id string → resolve to
    internal UUID via
         WorkflowRepository
         2. Query step packages with explicit filtering:
           - Use ArtifactRepository.list_for_workflow(workflow_id)
           - Filter explicitly: artifact_type == "step_package" AND pass_type
  ==
    PassType.EXECUTION
           - Group by step_number, select latest revision per step
         3. Validate each package against JSON schema:
           - Map step_number → domain.state.StepName (NOT
    infrastructure.db.models.StepName)
           - Load schema via
    application/schemas.get_schema_for_step(domain_step_name)
           - Validate package_type matches expected value (mismatch = INVALID)
           - Use jsonschema.Draft7Validator to validate content_jsonb
         4. Report status per package:
           - VALID - Package exists, correct type, passes schema validation
           - INVALID - Package exists but fails validation or wrong
  package_type
           - EMPTY - Package exists but content_jsonb is None
           - MISSING - No package found for step
         5. Exit 0 only if all requested steps have VALID packages

         Output Format

         Gate B Schema Validation Report
         ===============================
         Workflow: wf-abc123
         Steps: 1, 2, 3

         Step 1: problem_definition
         --------------------------
         Package: ProblemDefinitionPackage
         Revision: 1
         Status: VALID

         Step 2: requirements
         --------------------------
         Package: RequirementsPackage
         Revision: 2
         Status: INVALID
         Errors:
           - path: ['requirements', 0, 'id']
             message: 'INVALID-ID' does not match '^(FR|NFR|CR|IR)-\\d{3}$'

         Step 3: objectives
         --------------------------
         Status: MISSING

         Summary
         =======
         Expected: 3
         Valid:    1
         Invalid:  1
         Missing:  1
         Status:   FAIL

         Implementation Approach

         Self-Contained Design (Following verify_methodology.py Pattern)

         #!/usr/bin/env python3
         import sys, os
         sys.path.insert(0, os.path.join(os.path.dirname(__file__), "..",
  "apps",
    "api"))

         # Use domain.state.StepName for schema lookup (NOT
    infrastructure.db.models.StepName)
         from domain.state import StepName as DomainStepName
         from infrastructure.db.models import PassType
         from infrastructure.db.repositories import ArtifactRepository,
    WorkflowRepository
         from application.schemas import get_schema_for_step
         import jsonschema

         Step Number Mappings (Critical: Use domain.state.StepName)

         # Map step_number → domain.state.StepName for schema lookup
         STEP_NUMBER_TO_DOMAIN_STEP: Dict[int, DomainStepName] = {
             1: DomainStepName.PROBLEM_DEFINITION,
             2: DomainStepName.REQUIREMENTS,
             3: DomainStepName.OBJECTIVES,
         }

         # Expected package_type per step (for structural validation)
         EXPECTED_PACKAGE_TYPE: Dict[int, str] = {
             1: "ProblemDefinitionPackage",
             2: "RequirementsPackage",
             3: "ObjectivesPackage",
         }

         Key Query Logic (Explicit Filtering)

         # Fetch all artifacts and filter explicitly (transparent for
  verification
    tool)
         all_artifacts = await artifact_repo.list_for_workflow(workflow_uuid)

         # Filter to Pass 2 step packages only
         step_packages = [
             a for a in all_artifacts
             if a.artifact_type == "step_package"
             and a.pass_type == PassType.EXECUTION
             and a.step_number in steps
         ]

         # Group by step_number, get latest revision per step
         for step_num in steps:
             matches = [a for a in step_packages if a.step_number == step_num]

             if not matches:
                 status = "MISSING"
             else:
                 # Get latest revision
                 artifact = max(matches, key=lambda a: a.revision)

                 if artifact.content_jsonb is None:
                     status = "EMPTY"
                 elif artifact.package_type != EXPECTED_PACKAGE_TYPE[step_num]:
                     status = "INVALID"  # Wrong package type
                     errors = [{"message": f"Expected
    {EXPECTED_PACKAGE_TYPE[step_num]}, got
         {artifact.package_type}"}]
                 else:
                     # Validate against schema using domain.state.StepName
                     domain_step = STEP_NUMBER_TO_DOMAIN_STEP[step_num]
                     schema = get_schema_for_step(domain_step)
                     validator = jsonschema.Draft7Validator(schema)
                     errors =
  list(validator.iter_errors(artifact.content_jsonb))
                     status = "VALID" if not errors else "INVALID"

         Step-to-Schema Mapping

         | Step | StepName Enum      | Package Type             | Schema File
    |

    |------|--------------------|--------------------------|-------------------
  ---
    ----------|
         | 1    | PROBLEM_DEFINITION | ProblemDefinitionPackage |
    problem_definition.schema.json |
         | 2    | REQUIREMENTS       | RequirementsPackage      |
    requirements.schema.json       |
         | 3    | OBJECTIVES         | ObjectivesPackage        |
    objectives.schema.json         |

         Verification Commands

         # Help
         python tools/validate_schemas.py --help

         # Validate all steps for workflow
         python tools/validate_schemas.py --workflow-id wf-abc123

         # Verbose output with errors
         python tools/validate_schemas.py --workflow-id wf-abc123 --verbose

         # Specific steps only
         python tools/validate_schemas.py --workflow-id wf-abc123 --steps 1 2

         Dependencies (Read-Only)

         - apps/api/infrastructure/db/repositories/artifact.py -
    get_step_package()
         - apps/api/infrastructure/db/repositories/workflow.py -
    get_by_workflow_id()
         - apps/api/infrastructure/db/models/ - StepName, PassType
         - apps/api/application/schemas.py - get_schema_for_step()
         - packages/contracts/*.schema.json - Step 1-3 JSON schemas
         - apps/api/config.py - settings.postgres_url

         Not In Scope (Deferred per docs/DECISIONS.md)

         - tools/verify_traces.py - Traceability link verification
         - /workflows/{id}/traceability endpoint
         - Trace link validation (coverage_map, trace_map checks)

         Negative Patterns to Avoid (per Co-Developer-1)

         1. Don't mix StepName enums: Use domain.state.StepName for schema
  lookup,
    NOT
         infrastructure.db.models.StepName
         2. Don't skip explicit filtering: Use list_for_workflow() + explicit
    artifact_type/pass_type
         filters for transparency
         3. Don't implement deferred features: No traceability endpoint or
    verify_traces.py
         4. Don't default to "all workflows": Require explicit --workflow-id
         5. Don't bypass get_schema_for_step(): Use the application layer's
  schema
    loader, not hard-coded
         paths

         Design Decisions

         | Decision                        | Rationale
                 |

    |---------------------------------|----------------------------------------
  ---
    ---------------------
         --------|
         | --workflow-id required          | Verification tools should target
    explicit workflows
                 |
         | Package type mismatch = INVALID | Structural integrity matters;
  wrong
    type indicates data
         corruption     |
         | Latest revision only            | Gate B asks "does valid package
    exist?" not "are all revisions
         valid?" |
         | Explicit filtering              | Verification tool should be
    transparent about what it checks
                 |
         | EMPTY status added              | Distinct from MISSING (no
  artifact)
    and INVALID (failed
         validation)    |

         Test Scenarios

         1. --help shows usage
         2. Non-existent workflow returns exit code 3
         3. Workflow with valid packages returns exit code 0
         4. Workflow with missing packages returns exit code 1
         5. Workflow with invalid packages returns exit code 1 + errors in
  verbose
    mode
         6. Workflow with empty content_jsonb returns exit code 1 (EMPTY
  status)
         7. Workflow with wrong package_type returns exit code 1 (INVALID
  status)
    </information only> no action required.


• Understood. No action required.

─ Worked for 1m 19s ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

• Reviewed tools/validate_schemas.py against the approved P6.2 plan and
  directives. Overall aligned (explicit filtering, correct domain StepName
  mapping, package_type checks, no trace tooling), but there are a couple of
  spec/behavior mismatches to tighten.

  Findings

  - tools/validate_schemas.py:314 prints detailed errors even when --verbose is
    false because it always prints errors for INVALID statuses. The CLI spec
    says verbose mode controls detailed validation errors, so either gate errors
    strictly on --verbose or update the help text/spec to match current
    behavior.
  - tools/validate_schemas.py:408 uses exit code 2 for invalid step input, but
    the documented exit code 2 is “DB connection failed.” This conflates user
    input errors with DB failures; consider returning 1 or a dedicated code (or
    explicitly document the reuse).
  - docs/spec/0_SOLVER-Development-Directive.md shows python tools/
    validate_schemas.py --steps 1 2 3 without --workflow-id. The tool correctly
    requires an explicit workflow, but the doc mismatch should be noted for
    later correction.

  If you want, I can draft a short note to the Senior Developer to decide
  whether to align behavior with the CLI spec or update the spec text to match
  the current output.

    whether to align behavior with the CLI spec or update the spec text to

• Short note to Senior Developer:

  “P6.2 review: tools/validate_schemas.py prints detailed validation errors even
  without --verbose because if pkg.errors and (verbose or pkg.status ==
  STATUS_INVALID) (line ~314). The CLI spec says verbose controls detailed
  errors. Do you want to align behavior to the spec (only show errors with
  --verbose) or update the spec/help text to reflect the current always‑show
  errors for INVALID? Also note invalid step uses exit code 2 (same as DB
  error); if you keep it, please document the reuse.”


› The Senior Developer responded by doing: ⏺ Fixes applied:

  display with --verbose flag. Without it, users see just Status: INVALID (they
  can add -v for details).
    2. Exit code 2 documented: Updated docstring and plan to reflect that exit
  code 2 covers "Invalid arguments or database connection failed" - this
  documents the reuse for both invalid step numbers and DB errors.

   2. Add Scenario C3 Test (Revise Loop)

   Add to apps/api/tests/integration/test_orchestration_interrupt_resume.py:

   @pytest.mark.asyncio
   async def test_reject_revise_loops_back_to_review(self, checkpointer):
       """REJECT action triggers re-execution and returns to AWAITING_REVIEW.

       Gate C Scenario C3:
       GIVEN a workflow in awaiting_review status
       WHEN I apply REJECT (revise) with feedback
       THEN:
         - Workflow re-executes step with feedback context
         - Eventually returns to awaiting_review status
         - Step does NOT advance (still on same step)
       """
       graph = create_graph(checkpointer)
       thread_id = make_thread_id()
       checkpointer._test_thread_ids.append(thread_id)
       config = {"configurable": {"thread_id": thread_id}}

       initial_state = make_initial_state(thread_id, PassType.EXECUTION)

       # Run to interrupt at review
       result1 = await graph.ainvoke(initial_state, config)
       assert_interrupted(result1)
       assert result1["step_state"].status == StepStatus.AWAITING_REVIEW
       original_step = result1["current_step"]

       # Apply REJECT with feedback
       await graph.aupdate_state(
           config,
           {
               "human_decision": HumanAction.REJECT,
               "human_feedback": "Please add more detail to the analysis",
           },
           as_node="review",
       )

       # Resume - should re-execute and return to review
       result2 = await graph.ainvoke(None, config)

       # Should interrupt again at same step (re-execution complete)
       assert_interrupted(result2)
       assert result2["current_step"] == original_step  # Same step
       assert result2["step_state"].status == StepStatus.AWAITING_REVIEW

• I’m analyzing the Senior Dev’s approach to Gate C tests, noting it reuses
  existing integration tests and adds a make target—smart for scope and
  maintainability. However, their plan risks flaky, network-dependent tests
  since LLM calls and schema validations are invoked without stubbing; I’ll
  highlight necessary patches to keep tests deterministic and offline as best
  practice. I’ll also consider Makefile updates, explicit test coverage gaps,
  and dependency notes to provide a thorough perspective on improving test
  reliability and compliance with directives.

  Detailing test improvements and Makefile updates

  I’m focusing on key fixes needed for running make test-gates reliably in CI,
  especially stubbing LLM calls and validation to avoid flaky or hanging tests.
  I’ll also suggest adding test-gates to .PHONY and help outputs for Makefile
  correctness, plus recommend explicit gating scenario coverage and careful test
  selection to separate Gate C from Gate D. Finally, I'll outline best practices
  for isolating gating tests with monkeypatching to keep them fast and
  deterministic.

─ Worked for 1m 50s ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

• Findings

  - High: The planned tests execute the full graph and will call the real LLM
    adapter (apps/api/orchestration/nodes.py, apps/api/infrastructure/llm.py).
    That makes make test-gates depend on network/API keys and creates
    nondeterministic output that can fail schema validation and never reach
    awaiting_review. Gate C tests should stub generate_step_output and
    validate_output (or get_default_adapter) to keep them offline and
    deterministic.
  - High: The new “revise loop” test assumes a single resume returns to
    awaiting_review. Without stubbing validation to always pass, the graph can
    loop structure → validate indefinitely or fail before reaching review. This
    is a flake risk in CI.
  - Medium: make test-gates should be added to .PHONY and help output in
    Makefile to match existing targets and avoid “file target” surprises.
  - Medium: Scenario C2 (“cannot advance without approve”) is only inferred. If
    you want strict Appendix A coverage, add an explicit assertion that, absent
    an approve/revise action, the workflow does not advance (e.g., re‑invoke
    without setting human_decision and confirm current_step unchanged).
  - Low: Ensure test-gates selects only Gate C tests (not Gate D) so the target
    remains focused and fast; the current class selection is ok as long as it
    doesn’t broaden.

  Best path to achieve the objective

  - Keep the plan’s structure (add test-gates, add C3 test) but make the Gate C
    tests deterministic by monkeypatching
    orchestration.nodes.generate_step_output and
    orchestration.nodes.validate_output in apps/api/tests/integration/
    test_orchestration_interrupt_resume.py (or move Gate C tests to a dedicated
    apps/api/tests/integration/test_gate_c.py that applies the stubs). This
    preserves gate logic while removing network and schema‑content dependencies.
  - Add test-gates to .PHONY and help output in Makefile so the command is
    discoverable and behaves consistently.
  - If strict Appendix A adherence matters, add a minimal C2 assertion that “no
    approve → no advance” without relying on “message” to stand in for it.

• I mostly disagree with Co‑Developer‑2’s approach as written. It mirrors the
  Senior plan but misses a critical risk: these tests will call the real LLM
  adapter and schema validation, making make test-gates flaky and network/
  API‑key dependent, which violates the “avoid network access” directive and
  makes Gate C non‑deterministic. It also doesn’t address the Makefile hygiene
  (help + .PHONY) required by repo conventions.

  Key issues to flag to Senior Dev

  - Network/LLM dependency: The proposed test runs the full graph without
    stubbing generate_step_output/validate_output, which will hit
    get_default_adapter() and require OPENAI_API_KEY (apps/api/infrastructure/
    llm.py). This makes Gate C tests unreliable and not sandbox‑friendly.
  - Determinism: Without stubbing validation to always pass, the “revise loop”
    test may not return to awaiting_review consistently (could loop structure →
    validate or fail on schema).
  - Makefile hygiene: test-gates should be added to .PHONY and help text in
    Makefile so it’s discoverable and behaves consistently.
  - Coverage clarity: C2 (“cannot advance without approve”) is still inferred
    rather than asserted directly. It’s acceptable but not explicit.

  What I’d recommend instead

  - Keep the same file additions, but monkeypatch
    orchestration.nodes.generate_step_output and
    orchestration.nodes.validate_output in the Gate C tests to return a
    deterministic, schema‑valid payload. This isolates gate logic from LLM and
    schema complexities.
  - Add test-gates to .PHONY and help output in Makefile.
  - Optionally add a simple C2 assertion that a resume without setting
    human_decision leaves current_step unchanged.

  Negative patterns to avoid

  - Tests that implicitly require external API keys or network access for gate
    validation.
  - Gate targets that run broad integration suites beyond the gate’s scope.
  - Skipping Makefile help/.PHONY updates for new targets.

  Net: Co‑Dev‑2’s proposal is directionally aligned but incomplete and risky
  without stubbing and Makefile hygiene.

    Critical Analysis

    1. Monkeypatching LLM Calls - Co-Developer-1 is correct

    I missed this. The directive explicitly states:
    "Prefer deterministic tests; avoid external LLM calls or network access
  where possible."

    The existing tests in test_orchestration_interrupt_resume.py run
  graph.ainvoke() which executes structure_node → generate_step_output() (LLM
  call). Without stubbing, these tests either:
    - Require actual LLM access (violates directive)
    - Are flaky due to LLM variability
    - May pass accidentally due to error handling

    Verdict: Co-Developer-1's monkeypatch approach is essential for
  deterministic Gate C tests.

    2. New File vs. Extending Existing

    Co-Developer-1 proposes test_gate_c.py. Arguments:

    | For new file                         | For extending existing       |
    |--------------------------------------|------------------------------|
    | Clean separation of Gate C tests     | Avoids fixture duplication   |
    | Self-contained with monkeypatching   | Tests already exist and work |
    | Clearer make test-gates target       | Less code to maintain        |
    | Doesn't risk breaking existing tests | Consistent testing approach  |

    My perspective: A dedicated test_gate_c.py is cleaner IF it includes proper
  monkeypatching. The existing tests can remain as "smoke tests" that run with
  LLM (for P6.5 E2E perhaps), while the new file provides deterministic Gate C
  verification.

    3. Explicit C2 Test

    My plan treated C2 ("cannot advance without approve") as implied by C1
  (message doesn't advance) + the approve test. Co-Developer-1 adds an explicit
  test for "resuming without approve does not advance."

    Verdict: Explicit is better. The spec says "attempt to advance without
  calling /actions/approve" should fail. A direct test that tries to resume
  with no decision (or MESSAGE) and verifies no advancement is clearer.

    4. Graph-Level Focus

    Co-Developer-1 correctly identifies that Gate C tests should stay at graph
  level. Gate E (P6.5) covers API endpoints. This is the right separation:
    - Gate C: Proves the state machine prevents unauthorized advancement
    - Gate E: Proves the API correctly interfaces with that state machine

    Verdict: Agree with graph-level testing for Gate C.

    Answers to Co-Developer-1's Questions

    Q1: Should Gate C tests stay graph-level or hit API endpoints?

    Graph-level is correct. Gate C is about the state machine logic, not API
  behavior. The criterion "cannot advance without approve" is enforced by
  route_after_decision() in the graph, not by the API endpoints. Gate E should
  validate the API surface.

    Q2: New file or extend existing?

    I now prefer the new test_gate_c.py because:
    1. It allows clean monkeypatching without affecting existing tests
    2. make test-gates can target it precisely
    3. The existing tests serve a different purpose (full integration with LLM)

    Recommended Approach

    Accept Co-Developer-1's approach with these specifics:

    1. Create apps/api/tests/integration/test_gate_c.py with:
      - Monkeypatched generate_step_output returning deterministic output
      - Monkeypatched validate_output returning ValidationResult(passed=True)
      - Tests for C1, C2, C3
    2. Fixture setup in the new file (self-contained) or move common fixtures
  (checkpointer, make_thread_id, make_initial_state, assert_interrupted) to
  conftest.py
    3. make test-gates target should run ONLY the new test_gate_c.py:
    test-gates:
        cd apps/api && pytest tests/integration/test_gate_c.py -v
    4. Keep existing tests in test_orchestration_interrupt_resume.py as-is -
  they can serve as integration smoke tests for P6.5

    One Concern

    The monkeypatching needs to be carefully designed. The stubs must:
    - Return valid StepState with expected fields populated
    - Return ValidationResult compatible with what validate_node expects
    - Not break the graph's conditional edges

    Co-Developer-1 should specify exactly what the stub returns to ensure the
  graph traverses the expected path to review_node and triggers the interrupt.

    Summary

    Co-Developer-1's approach is better because:
    1. Addresses deterministic testing (I missed this)
    2. Provides explicit C2 test (my coverage was implicit)
    3. Clean file separation for focused make test-gates
    4. Correctly scopes graph-level testing (Gate E handles API)

    I recommend proceeding with Co-Developer-1's plan.

     1. Test Structure (follows Gate C pattern)

     """
     Gate D Integration Tests - Restart/Recovery

     P6.4: Tests Gate D criterion:
       "Restart + resume works mid-step and at awaiting_review with no lost
    artifacts."

     Uses monkeypatched LLM calls for deterministic, offline testing.
     """

     class TestGateDRestartRecovery:
         """Gate D: State survives restart; resume works correctly."""

         @pytest.mark.asyncio
         async def test_state_survives_restart(self, stub_llm_nodes):
             """D1: State persists across process restart (engine
    dispose/recreate)."""

         @pytest.mark.asyncio
         async def test_resume_at_awaiting_review(self, stub_llm_nodes):
             """D2: Resume works when workflow interrupted at
  awaiting_review."""

         @pytest.mark.asyncio
         async def test_resume_and_advance_after_restart(self, stub_llm_nodes):
             """D3: Can approve and advance workflow after restart."""

         @pytest.mark.asyncio
         async def test_artifacts_preserved_on_restart(self, stub_llm_nodes):
             """D4: Methodology dict/artifacts not lost on restart."""

     2. Restart Simulation Pattern

     # Phase 1: Run to interrupt with first checkpointer
     engine1 = create_async_engine(settings.postgres_url, echo=False)
     factory1 = async_sessionmaker(engine1, class_=AsyncSession,
    expire_on_commit=False)
     saver1 = SolverCheckpointSaver(factory1, engine=engine1)
     graph1 = create_graph(saver1)

     result1 = await graph1.ainvoke(initial_state, config)
     assert_interrupted(result1)

     # Dispose to simulate process death
     await engine1.dispose()

     # Phase 2: Create completely NEW everything, same thread_id
     engine2 = create_async_engine(settings.postgres_url, echo=False)
     factory2 = async_sessionmaker(engine2, class_=AsyncSession,
    expire_on_commit=False)
     saver2 = SolverCheckpointSaver(factory2, engine=engine2)
     graph2 = create_graph(saver2)

     # Verify state persisted
     snapshot = await graph2.aget_state(config)
     assert snapshot is not None, "State should persist after restart"

     3. Makefile Target

     test-recovery:

─ Worked for 1m 50s ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

• Findings (ordered by severity)

  - High: Gate D requires “conversation history preserved” after restart, but
    the plan doesn’t mention seeding or asserting WorkflowState.messages.
    Without explicitly inserting a non‑empty message before checkpointing and
    verifying it after restart, Gate D is incomplete. Reference: docs/
    spec/3_solver-technical-spec.md (Appendix A Gate D).
  - High: “mid-step” recovery is not in the Gate D scenario and may be brittle;
    LangGraph checkpoints are node‑boundary based. Testing mid‑step adds risk
    without spec coverage. Keep the test focused on awaiting_review (Step 2) per
    Gate D to avoid flaky behavior. Reference: docs/spec/3_solver-technical-
    spec.md.
  - Medium: The plan doesn’t explicitly ensure the workflow is at Step 2
    awaiting_review with Step 1 artifacts stored before restart. Gate D
    explicitly calls this out. The test setup should: run Step 1 → approve → run
    Step 2 → interrupt; then restart and assert current_step == requirements and
    artifacts includes Step 1 output. Reference: docs/spec/3_solver-technical-
    spec.md.
  - Medium: Gate D example uses POST /workflows/{id}/resume. A graph‑level test
    is acceptable if we explicitly note that API resume will be covered in Gate
    E; otherwise, the resume endpoint remains untested. Consider adding an
    API‑level resume check in P6.5 or call it out as a known gap. Reference:
    docs/spec/3_solver-technical-spec.md, docs/spec/0_SOLVER-Development-
    Directive.md.
  - Low: D1–D4 scenario labels aren’t in the spec. That’s fine if they map
    directly to the Gate D bullets, but avoid expanding scope (extra checks)
    beyond the spec without approval.

  What’s solid

  - Deterministic tests with monkeypatched LLM calls align with the “no network/
    LLM” requirement and follow the P6.3 pattern.
  - Using SolverCheckpointSaver and a full engine dispose/recreate cycle fits
    Decision #5 and Gate D intent.
  - Adding make test-recovery and updating .PHONY/help is correct and matches
    docs/spec/0_SOLVER-Development-Directive.md.

  Best path to meet the objective

  - Keep the test focused on the Gate D scenario: Step 2 awaiting_review, Step 1
    artifacts intact, messages preserved, resume continues.
  - Explicitly seed WorkflowState.messages with a non‑empty list before the
    first ainvoke, then assert it survives restart.
  - Structure the test setup so the restart happens after Step 2 reaches
    awaiting_review (ensures Step 1 artifacts exist).
  - If staying graph‑level only, add a note to cover POST /workflows/{id}/resume
    in P6.5; otherwise add a light API resume test to test-recovery.

  Open question

  - Do you want Gate D to assert persistence of WorkflowState.messages only, or
    also verify the messages table? (Graph‑level vs DB‑level persistence
    expectation).

• Consolidated Feedback for P6.4 (Gate D)
  You’re on the right track with a deterministic, graph‑level restart test that
  mirrors P6.3. The plan aligns with the repo’s verification philosophy
  (offline, no LLM calls, SolverCheckpointSaver). A few changes are needed to
  fully satisfy Gate D and keep spec fidelity.

  Must address

  - Conversation history preserved: Gate D explicitly calls out conversation
    history. Seed WorkflowState.messages before the first run and assert it
    survives the restart. Without this, Gate D is incomplete.
  - Step 2 awaiting_review + Step 1 artifacts: Gate D is explicit about Step 2
    awaiting_review with Step 1 artifacts intact. Structure the test so it
    reaches Step 2 review (by approving Step 1), then restart, then assert
    current_step == requirements, step_state.status == awaiting_review, and
    artifacts["problem_definition"] exists.
  - Avoid mid‑step recovery: The spec doesn’t require it and it’s brittle; keep
    the test scoped to the awaiting_review interrupt point.
  - Makefile consistency: Use the same invocation style as test-gates (currently
    PYTHONPATH=apps/api .venv/bin/pytest ...). Don’t introduce a different style
    for just this target unless you update both.

  Good decisions to keep

  - Dedicated apps/api/tests/integration/test_gate_d.py (avoids LLM‑dependent
    legacy tests)
  - Monkeypatching orchestration.nodes.generate_step_output and validate_output
  - Full restart simulation by disposing engine and recreating checkpointer/
    graph
  - SolverCheckpointSaver only

  <Senior Developer>
  Evaluation of Co-Developer-1's Feedback

    Analysis Against Directives and Spec

    Gate D Criterion (Doc 3 Appendix A): "Restart + resume works mid-step and
  at awaiting_review with no lost artifacts"

    Let me scrutinize Co-Developer-1's points:

    ---
    1. Conversation History Preserved

    Co-Developer-1's claim: Gate D explicitly calls out conversation history.

    My analysis: Looking at the spec, Gate D says "no lost artifacts" - not
  explicitly "conversation history." However, if WorkflowState.messages is part
  of the checkpointed state, testing its preservation is good practice even if
  not strictly required.

    Verdict: ✅ Agree - It's a reasonable extension. The criterion "no lost
  artifacts" could reasonably include workflow state messages. This makes the
  test more comprehensive.

    ---
    2. Step 2 awaiting_review + Step 1 Artifacts

    Co-Developer-1's claim: Structure the test to reach Step 2 review (by
  approving Step 1), then restart, then verify Step 1 artifacts are intact.

    My analysis: This is a stronger test design. My original plan tested
  restart at Step 1, which only proves state survives - not that prior step
  artifacts survive. Testing at Step 2 with Step 1 artifacts verifies:
    - Cross-step artifact preservation
    - Workflow advancement was correctly persisted
    - More realistic scenario (user approves, then process crashes)

    Verdict: ✅ Agree strongly - This aligns better with the "no lost
  artifacts" criterion.

    ---
    3. Avoid Mid-Step Recovery

    Co-Developer-1's claim: The spec doesn't require mid-step recovery and it's
  brittle.

    My analysis: Re-reading Gate D: "Restart + resume works mid-step and at
  awaiting_review..."

    Wait - the spec says "mid-step AND at awaiting_review". This suggests mid-
  step recovery IS part of Gate D.

    However, looking at the practical implementation: LangGraph checkpoints at
  interrupt points, not mid-generation. Mid-step recovery would require
  simulating a crash during LLM streaming, which is:
    - Hard to test deterministically
    - Depends on checkpoint frequency (not guaranteed mid-generation)
    - Brittle as Co-Developer-1 says

    Verdict: ⚖️ Partially agree - The spec mentions mid-step, but testing
  awaiting_review recovery is the practical, deterministic approach. Mid-step
  recovery is more of an infrastructure concern (LangGraph handles this). I'll
  defer to Co-Developer-1's pragmatic approach but note the spec does mention
  it.

    ---
    4. Makefile Consistency

    Co-Developer-1's claim: Use the same invocation style as test-gates.

    My analysis: My plan already shows PYTHONPATH=apps/api .venv/bin/pytest
  apps/api/tests/integration/test_gate_d.py -v which matches test-gates. This
  is consistent.

    Verdict: ✅ Already aligned - No change needed.

    ---
    Co-Developer-1's Questions

    Q1: For "conversation history preserved," should we only verify
  WorkflowState.messages, or also assert persistence in the messages table?

    My answer: Gate D is about checkpoint persistence (state survives restart).
  WorkflowState.messages in the checkpoint is the primary concern. The messages
  table is separate DB persistence (audit/retrieval) - that's more of a Gate F
  concern. Focus on checkpoint state only.

    Q2: Should test-recovery run only the deterministic Gate D file, or also
  include test_orchestration_interrupt_resume.py?

    My answer: Only the deterministic file. The existing tests in
  test_orchestration_interrupt_resume.py may have LLM dependencies. Mixing them
  could cause flaky tests or require LLM keys. Keep test-recovery scoped to
  test_gate_d.py only - this follows the Gate C pattern where test-gates only
  runs test_gate_c.py.

    ---
    My Perspective: What's Best

    Co-Developer-1's feedback improves my plan in meaningful ways:

    | Original Plan            | Co-Developer-1's Improvement         | Benefit
  |

  |--------------------------|--------------------------------------|----------
  ------------------------|
    | 4 separate tests (D1-D4) | 1-2 focused comprehensive tests      | Less
  duplication, clearer intent |
    | Test at Step 1           | Test at Step 2 with Step 1 artifacts | Proves
  cross-step preservation   |
    | D3: mid-step recovery    | Drop mid-step recovery               |
  Deterministic, not brittle       |
    | No message testing       | Seed and verify messages             | More
  complete state verification |

    I recommend adopting Co-Developer-1's approach with these specific
  implementations:

    1. Single comprehensive test: test_restart_preserves_state_and_artifacts
      - Build to Step 2 awaiting_review (approve Step 1 first)
      - Seed messages before run
      - Restart (dispose engine, new checkpointer/graph)
      - Assert: current_step, status, Step 1 artifacts, messages
    2. Second test for resume verification: test_resume_continues_after_restart
      - After restart, approve Step 2
      - Verify workflow advances to Step 3 or completes

    This gives us:
    - ✅ Complete Gate D coverage
    - ✅ Deterministic tests
    - ✅ Cross-step artifact verification
    - ✅ Message preservation
    - ✅ Resume continuation proof

       P6.4 Plan: Gate D Test (Restart/Recovery) - Revised

       Objective

       Create make test-recovery target with deterministic Gate D tests
  following the Gate C pattern.

       Gate D Criterion (Doc 3 Appendix A): "Restart + resume works mid-step
  and at awaiting_review with
       no lost artifacts"

       Approach (Revised per Co-Developer-1)

       1. New dedicated file apps/api/tests/integration/test_gate_d.py -
  follows Gate C pattern
       2. Monkeypatch LLM calls - reuse stub_llm_nodes pattern from
  test_gate_c.py
       3. Test at Step 2 awaiting_review - Run to Step 1 review → approve → run
  to Step 2 review → restart
       4. Verify Step 1 artifacts preserved - Cross-step artifact preservation
  is key
       5. Seed and verify messages - WorkflowState.messages should survive
  restart
       6. No mid-step recovery - Focus on awaiting_review checkpoint
  (deterministic)
       7. Simulate process death - dispose engine, create new checkpointer/
  graph instances

       Files

       | File                                      | Action | Purpose
            |
       |-------------------------------------------|--------|------------------
  ---------------------------
       -----|
       | apps/api/tests/integration/test_gate_d.py | Create | Deterministic
  Gate D tests with
       monkeypatching   |
       | Makefile                                  | Edit   | Add test-recovery
  target, update .PHONY and
       help |

       Test Scenarios (Revised)

       | ID     | Scenario                         | Test
  |

  |--------|----------------------------------|--------------------------------
  ------------|
       | D1-D4  | Comprehensive restart/recovery   |
  test_restart_preserves_state_and_artifacts |
       | Resume | Workflow continues after restart |
  test_resume_continues_after_restart        |

       Key Design Decisions

       1. 2 focused tests instead of 4 separate - comprehensive coverage, less
  duplication
       2. Test at Step 2 - Verifies cross-step artifact preservation (Step 1
  artifacts survive)
       3. Seed and verify messages - Gate D explicitly requires "conversation
  history preserved"
       4. SolverCheckpointSaver only - per DECISIONS.md
       5. Only run test_gate_d.py - Don't include legacy tests with LLM
  dependencies

       Mid-Step Recovery: Pragmatic Limitation

       Spec says: "Restart + resume works mid-step and at awaiting_review"

       Limitation: Mid-step recovery is not tested in test_gate_d.py due to:
       - LangGraph checkpoints at interrupt boundaries, not mid-generation
       - Simulating a crash during LLM streaming is non-deterministic
       - Would require mocking checkpoint timing or process signals

       Pragmatic approach: Focus on awaiting_review checkpoints which:
       - Are the primary human interaction points
       - Have deterministic state
       - Cover the "no lost artifacts" criterion

       This is acknowledged as a partial Gate D implementation. If full mid-
  step testing is required,
       invoke deviation protocol or extend with integration tests that use real
  LLM calls
       (non-deterministic).

       Implementation

       1. Test Structure

       """
       Gate D Integration Tests - Restart/Recovery

       P6.4: Tests Gate D criterion:
         "Restart + resume works mid-step and at awaiting_review with no lost
  artifacts."

       Uses monkeypatched LLM calls for deterministic, offline testing.
       Graph-level tests only (Gate E covers API endpoints).
       """

       class TestGateDRestartRecovery:
           """Gate D: State survives restart; artifacts and messages
  preserved."""

           @pytest.mark.asyncio
           async def test_restart_preserves_state_and_artifacts(self,
  stub_llm_nodes):
               """D1-D4: State, artifacts, and messages survive process
  restart.

               GIVEN a workflow at Step 2 awaiting_review with Step 1 artifacts
  and messages
               WHEN process restarts (engine disposed, new checkpointer/graph
  created)
               THEN current_step == REQUIREMENTS
               AND step_state.status == AWAITING_REVIEW
               AND methodology["problem_definition"] exists (Step 1 artifact)
               AND messages preserved
               """

           @pytest.mark.asyncio
           async def test_resume_continues_after_restart(self, stub_llm_nodes):
               """Resume: Workflow can continue after restart.

               GIVEN a workflow restarted at Step 2 awaiting_review
               WHEN I approve Step 2 after restart
               THEN workflow advances to Step 3
               """

       2. Test Flow (test_restart_preserves_state_and_artifacts)

       # Phase 1: Build to Step 2 awaiting_review
       engine1 = create_async_engine(settings.postgres_url, echo=False)
       factory1 = async_sessionmaker(engine1, class_=AsyncSession,
  expire_on_commit=False)
       saver1 = SolverCheckpointSaver(factory1, engine=engine1)
       graph1 = create_graph(saver1)

       # Initial state with seeded messages
       initial_state = WorkflowState(
           ...,
           messages=[Message(role="user", content="Initial problem
  description")],
       )

       # Run to Step 1 review
       result1 = await graph1.ainvoke(initial_state, config)
       assert_interrupted(result1)
       assert result1["current_step"] == StepName.PROBLEM_DEFINITION

       # Approve Step 1 → advance to Step 2 review
       await graph1.aupdate_state(config, {"human_decision":
  HumanAction.APPROVE}, as_node="review")
       result2 = await graph1.ainvoke(None, config)
       assert_interrupted(result2)
       assert result2["current_step"] == StepName.REQUIREMENTS
       assert "problem_definition" in result2.get("methodology", {})

       # Dispose to simulate process death
       await engine1.dispose()

       # Phase 2: Create completely NEW everything, same thread_id
       engine2 = create_async_engine(settings.postgres_url, echo=False)
       factory2 = async_sessionmaker(engine2, class_=AsyncSession,
  expire_on_commit=False)
       saver2 = SolverCheckpointSaver(factory2, engine=engine2)
       graph2 = create_graph(saver2)

       # Verify state persisted
       snapshot = await graph2.aget_state(config)
       assert snapshot is not None, "State should persist after restart"
       assert snapshot.values["current_step"] == StepName.REQUIREMENTS.value
       assert snapshot.values["step_state"]["status"] ==
  StepStatus.AWAITING_REVIEW.value
       assert "problem_definition" in snapshot.values.get("methodology", {})
       # Messages verification (if in checkpoint state)

       3. Makefile Target

       test-recovery:
       	@echo "Running Gate D tests (restart/recovery)..."
       	PYTHONPATH=apps/api .venv/bin/pytest apps/api/tests/integration/
  test_gate_d.py -v
       	@echo "✓ Gate D tests passed"

       Implementation Steps

       1. Create apps/api/tests/integration/test_gate_d.py:
         - Copy fixture pattern from test_gate_c.py (stub_llm_nodes,
  checkpointer)
         - Implement 2 focused test scenarios
         - Use restart simulation pattern (engine dispose/recreate)
         - Test at Step 2 with Step 1 artifacts
       2. Update Makefile:
         - Add .PHONY: test-recovery
         - Add help text: make test-recovery   - Run Gate D tests (restart/
  recovery)
         - Add test-recovery target after test-gates
       3. Run make test-recovery to verify

       Verification

       make test-recovery

       # Expected:
       # tests/integration/
  test_gate_d.py::TestGateDRestartRecovery::test_restart_preserves_state_and_ar
  ti
       facts PASSED
       # tests/integration/
  test_gate_d.py::TestGateDRestartRecovery::test_resume_continues_after_restart
       PASSED
       # ✓ Gate D tests passed

       Negative Patterns to Avoid

       1. No real LLM calls - tests must be deterministic and offline
       2. Use SolverCheckpointSaver - not PostgresSaver (per DECISIONS.md)
       3. Don't mix Gate C/E concerns - test-recovery is Gate D only
       4. No API endpoints - graph-level only (Gate E covers API)
       5. Don't include legacy tests - Only run test_gate_d.py, not
  test_orchestration_interrupt_resume.py
       6. No mid-step recovery - Focus on awaiting_review checkpoint points

       Exit Criteria

       - make test-recovery exits with code 0
       - Tests verify: state survives, artifacts preserved, messages preserved,
  resume works
       - Tests run without LLM/network access
       - Tests prove restart doesn't lose state or artifacts

       Dependencies

       - PostgreSQL running (make dev-db)
       - No LLM API keys required (monkeypatched)
  </information only> no action required

  next instruction.

   async def test_sse_stream_connects(self, client):
       # Create workflow first
       create_resp = await client.post(API_PREFIX, json={
           "problem": "Test SSE", "created_by": "test-actor"
       })
       workflow_id = create_resp.json()["workflow_id"]

       # Connect to SSE stream
       async with client.stream("GET", f"{API_PREFIX}/{workflow_id}/stream") as
  response:
           assert response.status_code == 200
           assert "text/event-stream" in response.headers["content-type"]

           # Read first event (initial snapshot)
           async for line in response.aiter_lines():
               if line.startswith("data:"):
                   event_data = json.loads(line[5:].strip())
                   assert "event_type" in event_data
                   break

• I partially disagree with the plan as written. It’s on the right path
  (HTTP‑level tests, ASGITransport, no external server/LLM), but it doesn’t
  actually satisfy Gate E as specified.

  Key gaps vs directives/spec:

  - Gate E requires a full SSE event sequence (workflow.started → step.started →
    artifact.delta → artifact.final → step.awaiting_review, then step.approved →
    next step) per docs/spec/3_solver-technical-spec.md. “Test what exists and
    document gaps” is not acceptable for an acceptance gate; it needs
    implementation or a deviation entry in docs/DECISIONS.md.
  - The current SSE endpoint only yields a snapshot + heartbeat (apps/api/
    routes/workflows.py); the plan doesn’t add real event emission, so tests
    won’t be able to assert the required event order.
  - The plan reuses the Gate C stub, which returns Pass 2 outputs. POST /
    workflows starts Pass 1 (definition), so a Pass‑2‑style stub will likely
    mis‑align with Pass‑1 validation/flow and can cause loops or invalid state.
  - Spec/README require make e2e, but Makefile only has test-e2e. Gate E should
    add an e2e target or alias in Makefile to match the spec.

  Best path to meet the objective:

  - Implement SSE event emission, not just tests. Add a small in‑memory broker
    (queue + backlog per workflow) and publish events from the API layer after
    graph runs. Keep it minimal (no new architecture beyond a tiny helper in
    apps/api/routes/workflows.py or a small module under apps/api/application/).
  - Update /workflows/{id}/stream to replay backlog then stream live events with
    keep‑alive comments. This allows the Gate E flow to work even if the stream
    connects after workflow creation.
  - Make the LLM stub pass‑aware: return methodology payloads for Pass 1 and
    package payloads for Pass 2; override validate_output to always pass in
    tests so the flow is deterministic.
  - Add an e2e Makefile target (or alias to test-e2e) to satisfy the command in
    docs/spec/0_SOLVER-Development-Directive.md and README.md.

  Negative patterns to avoid:

  - Treating missing SSE events as “acceptable gaps” instead of implementing or
    logging a deviation.
  - Using a Pass‑2 stub for Pass‑1 execution in API tests.
  - Relying on TestClient for SSE without streamed line parsing + explicit stop
    conditions (risk of hanging).
  - Expanding Gate E to cover revise/message beyond what the spec requires if it
    adds flakiness.

  If you want, I can provide a concrete event‑broker sketch and a tighter Gate E
  test matrix tailored to apps/api/routes/workflows.py and the existing graph
  behavior.

  Event‑Broker Sketch (Minimal, In‑Process, Backlog + Live)
